   180	const asg = (platform, role, model, effort = null) => ({ platform, role, model, effort, mode: platform === 'codex' ? 'native' : 'delegated' });
   181	
   182	// The full read-only review seat set (must ALL be read-only on both platforms —
   183	// final-reviewer-b included; verifier iter-2 finding).
   184	const REVIEWER_ROLES = ['plan-reviewer', 'test-verifier', 'subtask-reviewer', 'final-reviewer-a', 'final-reviewer-b'];
   185	
   186	const valAfter = (/** @type {string[]} */ args, /** @type {string} */ flag) => {
   187	  const i = args.indexOf(flag);
   188	  return i >= 0 ? args[i + 1] : undefined;
   189	};
   190	// A claude child is WRITE-CAPABLE if it auto-accepts edits, allows Write/Edit,
   191	// or allows a Bash entry that is NOT a scoped read-only git command. A reviewer
   192	// may carry `Bash(git diff:*)` / `Bash(git log:*)` / `Bash(git show:*)` /
   193	// `Bash(git status:*)` for diff inspection and still be read-only (G5).
   194	const READONLY_GIT_BASH = /^Bash\(git (diff|log|show|status)[):]/;
   195	const claudeGrantsWrite = (/** @type {string[]} */ args) => {
   196	  if (valAfter(args, '--permission-mode') === 'acceptEdits') return true;
   197	  const tools = valAfter(args, '--allowedTools') ?? '';
   198	  if (/\bWrite\b|\bEdit\b/.test(tools)) return true;
   199	  const bash = tools.split(',').map((t) => t.trim()).filter((t) => /^Bash\b/.test(t));
   200	  return bash.some((t) => !READONLY_GIT_BASH.test(t));
   201	};
   202

    55	docs/plans/2026-07-11-baton-v1.md:58:**Concurrency** (Gate-1 additions, iterations 1+2): all bundle mutations run under a repo-scoped lock — `.handoff/lock` acquired via atomic `mkdir`/`O_EXCL`, containing `{writerId: <platform>-<pid>-<sessionHint>, fencingToken: <random per acquisition>, acquiredAt, heartbeatAt}`. **Lock model (iterations 3+4)**: mutation locks are **operation-scoped** — acquired for the duration of one command's mutation and released, never held across turns; logical session ownership lives in `bundle.json` (the active `origin`/generation), not in the lock. Lock metadata (host, pid, process start time, fencing token) is written atomically with acquisition; a crash between lock creation and metadata publication reads as torn metadata. Recovery rules, conservative on every OS: automatic recovery only when the owner is **provably dead** (same host, pid gone or pid/start-time mismatch); a same-host owner confirmed alive is **refused** — including `recover --force` — with the instruction to terminate that process first; missing/torn/cross-host/unverifiable owner metadata is treated as **unsupported for automatic recovery** (refusal with explanation), never as safe-to-steal. Fencing tokens remain a fast-abort layer. Tests: force attempted against a live paused writer (refused), crash between lock creation and metadata publication, owner paused after its final token check, torn-metadata refusal. Journal entries carry `writerId`; sequence numbers are allocated under the lock. **Semantic isolation**: v1 enforces one active task — a checkpoint whose `sessionHint`/origin doesn't match the active bundle is rejected with an actionable warning (`--take-over` supersedes: archives the current bundle to `history/` and starts fresh), so two unrelated sessions can't merge goals/files/decisions into one handoff. `receive` is a core-owned two-phase transaction. `receive --prepare` validates + resolves roles + returns a one-time receipt token and the resume prompt without mutating state; the token **binds** the bundle revision (journalSeq), normalized intake, config digest, target platform+session, the probe snapshot used for remap, **and a content-sensitive prepare-time git digest** — HEAD + branch + hashes of the staged and unstaged diffs + a bounded untracked-files digest (path + size + content hash, capped) — so a file that stays `M` but changes content still invalidates the token. `--commit` re-derives and compares every bound input, rejecting any drift (intervening checkpoint, config edit, HEAD move, content change, probe change) and requiring re-prepare; each declared drift input has its own rejection test. `receive --commit <token>` performs one atomic transition: `sealed → received` (normal) or `open → received` (the primary failure case: limit death before finalize; commit writes a receive-generated degraded seal recording intake origin/reason, flagged `degraded-seal`) — and in the same transition **adopts the receiving platform/session as the bundle's active owner and opens a fresh writable generation** (`generation` counter incremented, `status: open`), archiving the prior seal + receipt to `history/`. The receiving session's subsequent checkpoints are therefore first-party, and the bundle is immediately receivable again if the new platform also dies — chained A→B→C failover is contract-tested with checkpoints fired immediately after each receive. Cancellation or a competing receiver leaves state untouched; double-commit fails cleanly. Identical semantics on every adapter; six-direction contract tests cover sealed + unsealed variants. Tests: concurrent writers, provably-dead takeover, fencing-token abort incl. pause-after-final-check, foreign-session rejection/take-over, prepare-vs-checkpoint races, prepare-without-commit, double-commit, competing receivers, chained failovers.

    93	/**
    94	 * Scripted fake child runner honoring the injection seam. Each call shifts the
    95	 * next result off the queue, writes its logContent to opts.logPath (so the
    96	 * command reads the transcript from the child's LOG file), and records the
    97	 * spawn spec.
    98	 */
    99	function fakeRunner(io, script) {
   100	  const queue = [...script];
   101	  const calls = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
   102	  const fn = (/** @type {any} */ spec, /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) => {
   103	    calls.push({ spec, opts, args: spec?.args ?? [], command: spec?.command, env: spec?.env });
   104	    const r = queue.shift() ?? { timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '' };
   105	    // No fallback: the command MUST pass a real logPath (asserted below).
   106	    const logPath = opts.logPath;
   107	    if (typeof r.logContent === 'string') {
   108	      const dir = logPath.slice(0, logPath.lastIndexOf('/'));
   109	      io.fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
   110	      io.fs.writeFileSync(logPath, r.logContent);
   111	    }
   112	    return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: r.timedOut ?? false, exitCode: r.exitCode ?? 0, verdict: r.verdict ?? 'APPROVED', findings: r.findings ?? '', logPath });
   113	  };
   114	  fn.calls = calls;
   115	  return fn;
   116	}

   280	  function smokeExecFile(rec) {
   281	    return (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = []) => {
   282	      rec.push([cmd, ...args].join(' '));
   283	      // git stays unavailable; the smoke command "passes".
   284	      if (cmd === 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   285	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   286	    };
   287	  }

   328	    // A git-aware execFile whose HEAD is a mutable ref — HEAD is a bound token
   329	    // input, so moving it between issuance and approval is genuine drift.
   330	    let head = 'HEAD-A';
   331	    const gitAware = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = []) => {
   332	      if (cmd === 'git') {
   333	        const key = args.join(' ');
   334	        const out = key === 'rev-parse HEAD' ? head : key === 'rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD' ? 'main' : '';
   335	        return Promise.resolve({ stdout: out + '\n', stderr: '' });
   336	      }
   337	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' }); // the smoke command
   338	    };
   339	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ spec: smokeSpec, execFile: gitAware, runner: undefined });
   340	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
   341	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   342	    assert.equal(await cmdLoop(['run'], io), 0);
   343	    const token = JSON.parse(loopFile(io, 'smoke-approval.json')).token; // the ACTUAL issued smk1 token
   344	
   345	    // Drift a bound input: HEAD moves after the human looked.
   346	    head = 'HEAD-B';
   347	    const runner2 = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
   348	    io.superviseChild = runner2;
   349	    io.__runner = runner2;
   350	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run', '--approve-smoke', token], io); // the OLD token, now stale
   351	    assert.equal(code, 4, 'the plan parks on smoke-token drift');
   352	    assert.equal(runner2.calls.length, 0, 'a drifted approval spawns no child');
   353	    const { state } = await loadLoopState('/repo', io);
   354	    assert.equal(state.status, LOOP_STATUS.PARKED, 'drift parks the run (not left silently awaiting)');
   355	  });

   476	
   477	// ===========================================================================
   478	// B8 (G2) — the run-lock acquisition must be ATOMIC, not check-then-write.
   479	describe('loop run — atomic run-lock acquisition (B8)', () => {
   480	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
   481	  const LOCK = `${DIR}/supervisor.lock`;
   482	
   483	  it('RED (B8): implementation-neutral — the competitor materializes AT the exclusive-create; wx/mkdir-first refuses, check-then-write clobbers (RED)', async () => {
   484	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
   485	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
   486	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
   487	    io.processAlive = (p) => p === 55555; // the competitor supervisor is LIVE
   488	
   489	    const realWrite = io.fs.writeFileSync.bind(io.fs);
   490	    const realMkdir = io.fs.mkdirSync.bind(io.fs);
   491	    const realRename = io.fs.renameSync.bind(io.fs);
   492	    const COMPETITOR = JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 55555, startTime: 222, runId: 'competitor' });
   493	
   494	    // The competitor wins the lock the INSTANT before the supervisor's own write
   495	    // to LOCK lands — injected at whichever primitive the acquire uses to create
   496	    // it. NO readFileSync hook, so a wx/mkdir-FIRST acquire (which never reads
   497	    // before creating) cannot dodge the race.
   498	    let injected = false;
   499	    const injectBefore = (/** @type {any} */ target) => {
   500	      if (injected || String(target) !== LOCK) return;
   501	      injected = true;
   502	      realMkdir(DIR, { recursive: true });
   503	      realWrite(LOCK, COMPETITOR);
   504	    };
   505	
   506	    // Exclusive create (flag wx/ax) enforces the O_EXCL semantics memfs lacks:
   507	    // once the competitor exists, an exclusive write throws EEXIST.
   508	    io.fs.writeFileSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ data, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
   509	      const flag = typeof opts === 'string' ? undefined : opts?.flag;
   510	      const exclusive = flag === 'wx' || flag === 'ax' || flag === 'wx+' || flag === 'ax+';
   511	      injectBefore(p);
   512	      if (exclusive && String(p) === LOCK && io.fs.existsSync(p)) throw Object.assign(new Error(`EEXIST: file already exists, open '${p}'`), { code: 'EEXIST' });
   513	      return realWrite(p, data, opts); // a PLAIN write to LOCK clobbers the competitor
   514	    };
   515	    // mkdirSync(LOCK) as an exclusive lock dir: memfs already throws EEXIST when a
   516	    // node exists at the path (the injected competitor file).
   517	    io.fs.mkdirSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ opts) => { injectBefore(p); return realMkdir(p, opts); };
   518	    // A tmp+rename publish (atomicWriteJson) clobbers the competitor on rename.
   519	    io.fs.renameSync = (/** @type {any} */ from, /** @type {any} */ to) => { injectBefore(to); return realRename(from, to); };
   520	
   521	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
   522	    assert.equal(code, 1, 'a wx/mkdir-first exclusive acquire observes EEXIST and refuses the run');
   523	    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned when the lock was lost to the competitor');
   524	    assert.match(JSON.parse(io.files()[LOCK]).runId, /competitor/, "the competitor's lock is not clobbered (a check-then-write path fails here)");
   525	  });
   526	});
   527	
   528	// ===========================================================================
   529	// G4 (A4) — record in-flight child groups; kill recorded orphans on reclaim.
   530	describe('loop run — child-group recording + kill-on-reclaim (G4)', () => {
   531	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
   532	
   533	  const readChildren = (io) => {
   534	    const raw = io.files()[`${DIR}/children.ndjson`];
   535	    if (typeof raw !== 'string') return [];
   536	    return raw.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== '').map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
   537	  };
   538	
   539	  it('RED (G4): a child-start record {childId, pid, pgid, startedAt} exists on disk WHILE the child is in flight (before completion)', async () => {
   540	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
   541	    let n = 0;
   542	    const inflightOk = [];
   543	    // The runner reports the child's group via opts.onStart (spawn time); the
   544	    // supervisor must persist the record synchronously in that handler — BEFORE
   545	    // the child completes — so a mid-run crash leaves the orphan reap-able.
   546	    io.superviseChild = (/** @type {any} */ _spec, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
   547	      n += 1;
   548	      const info = { pid: 8000 + n, pgid: 9000 + n };
   549	      if (typeof opts.onStart === 'function') opts.onStart(info);
   550	      const rec = readChildren(io).find((r) => r.pgid === info.pgid);
   551	      inflightOk.push(
   552	        !!rec && typeof rec.childId === 'string' && rec.childId.length > 0 && rec.pid === info.pid && typeof rec.startedAt === 'string' && rec.startedAt.length > 0,
   553	      );
   554	      return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath, ...info });
   555	    };
   556	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
   557	    assert.equal(code, 0);
   558	    assert.ok(inflightOk.length >= 1, 'at least one child ran');
   559	    assert.ok(inflightOk.every(Boolean), 'each child-start record ({childId,pid,pgid,startedAt}) existed on disk while its child was in flight');
   560	  });

[high] — The raw-deleted worktree pin’s fixture does not actually make `git worktree list` include `wt-a`: `list()` emits only entries from `added` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:94), but the test calls only `setBranch`, which mutates `branchAt` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:144). It does not require `children.ndjson`, `childId`, `startedAt`, or before-spawn/before-completion ordering. — Fix: assert the exact `children.ndjson` record shape and add an ordering seam that proves the record exists while the child is still in flight.

5. [high] — The dead-lock reclaim kill pin does not prove kill-before-resume ordering. It seeds `children.ndjson` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:545) and checks a kill happened by the end at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:549), but a wrong implementation could spawn/resume first and kill later. — Fix: make the fake runner assert the orphan group was killed before its first spawn, and record ordered events for `processKill` vs `superviseChild`.

6. [medium] — The B8 race pin is overfit to a read-based implementation. It injects the competitor only inside `io.fs.readFileSync(LOCK)` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:495), while a valid exclusive `mkdir`/`open wx` implementation could acquire without reading that path and would not trigger the test’s competitor. Current code does have the check/read then write shape at [loop.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/loop.mjs:142) and [loop.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/loop.mjs:162), so the red is meaningful against today’s code but not implementation-neutral. — Fix: move the race injection to the create/rename primitive or add an explicit exclusive-create companion that passes for `wx`/`mkdir` and fails for check-then-write.

The second run then collides with fake branch state from `checkout -b` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:122), so it fails for stale-branch reasons, not cap refund. — Fix: leave a deliberate running/parked/crash-resumable state with exactly 3 persisted iterations, then resume and assert attempts 4/5 persist and attempt 6 escalates.

3. [high] — The raw-deleted worktree pin’s fixture does not actually make `git worktree list` include `wt-a`: `list()` emits only entries from `added` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:94), but the test calls only `setBranch`, which mutates `branchAt` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:144). The test comment claims the list includes `wt-a` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:674), but the fake does not model that. — Fix: add an explicit fake-git seed for listed worktrees, with missing cwd behavior that crashes unless `selfHealWorktree` prunes and re-adds.

4. [high] — The G4 child-start pin is too weak for the finding. The finding requires child-start `{pid, pgid}` journaling before recovery needs it at [findings.md](/Users/saksham/baton/reviews/2026-07-19-gate-2-milestone-b/findings.md:40), but the test accepts any file under `.handoff/loop/` containing a `pgid` after the fake runner has already returned at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:521) and [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:531). It does not require `children.ndjson`, `childId`, `startedAt`, or before-spawn/before-completion ordering. — Fix: assert the exact `children.ndjson` record shape and add an ordering seam that proves the record exists while the child is still in flight.

5. [high] — The dead-lock reclaim kill pin does not prove kill-before-resume ordering. It seeds `children.ndjson` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:545) and checks a kill happened by the end at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:549), but a wrong implementation could spawn/resume first and kill later. — Fix: make the fake runner assert the orphan group was killed before its first spawn, and record ordered events for `processKill` vs `superviseChild`.

6. [medium] — The B8 race pin is overfit to a read-based implementation. It injects the competitor only inside `io.fs.readFileSync(LOCK)` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:495), while a valid exclusive `mkdir`/`open wx` implementation could acquire without reading that path and would not trigger the test’s competitor. Current code does have the check/read then write shape at [loop.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/loop.mjs:142) and [loop.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/loop.mjs:162), so the red is meaningful against today’s code but not implementation-neutral. — Fix: move the race injection to the create/rename primitive or add an explicit exclusive-create companion that passes for `wx`/`mkdir` and fails for check-then-write.

7. [medium] — G5’s reviewer-blindness sub-item is not fully pinned. The finding requires the reviewer prompt to review `main..branch` via git at [findings.md](/Users/saksham/baton/reviews/2026-07-19-gate-2-milestone-b/findings.md:49), but the tests only identify reviewer calls by the prompt containing `subtask-reviewer` at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:201) and assert model/cwd/read-only at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:323). — Fix: assert the reviewer prompt contains an explicit `git diff main..baton/wt-*/subtask-*` or equivalent `main..branch` instruction.

8. [low] — `claudeGrantsWrite` still fails open for write-capable Claude tools that are not literal `Write` or standalone `Edit`: the helper only checks acceptEdits, `Write`/`Edit`, and non-scoped Bash at [loop-children.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-children.test.mjs:53), and the no-write assertion reuses the same narrow `Write|Edit` test at [loop-children.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/loop-children.test.mjs:113). — Fix: fail closed by allowlisting known read-only tools, or explicitly reject all known write/edit tool names including composite edit tools.

codex
The current B8 test no longer has a `readFileSync` race hook. The injection is on `writeFileSync(LOCK)`, `mkdirSync(LOCK)`, and `renameSync(..., LOCK)`, which is the right surface to distinguish exclusive-create-first from check-then-write if the wrappers behave as intended.

     1	// In-memory fs fake for unit tests. Synchronous subset only.
     2	// Atomicity contract: renameSync moves content in a single step, so a target
     3	// path is never observed carrying partial content. Every mutation appends a
     4	// full snapshot to `fs.__history`, letting tests enumerate intermediate states
     5	// and prove the tmp+rename discipline (target's first appearance is a rename).
     6	
     7	function fsError(code, syscall, path) {
     8	  const e = new Error(`${code}: ${syscall} '${path}'`);
     9	  e.code = code;
    10	  e.syscall = syscall;
    11	  e.path = path;
    12	  return e;
    13	}
    14	
    15	function normalize(p) {
    16	  const str = String(p);
    17	  const isAbs = str.startsWith('/');
    18	  const out = [];
    19	  for (const part of str.split('/')) {
    20	    if (part === '' || part === '.') continue;
    21	    if (part === '..') {
    22	      out.pop();
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    out.push(part);
    26	  }
    27	  return (isAbs ? '/' : '') + out.join('/');
    28	}
    29	
    30	function dirname(np) {
    31	  const i = np.lastIndexOf('/');
    32	  if (i <= 0) return '/';
    33	  return np.slice(0, i);
    34	}
    35	
    36	function basename(np, parent) {
    37	  return parent === '/' ? np.slice(1) : np.slice(parent.length + 1);
    38	}
    39	
    40	export function makeMemfs(initialFiles = {}) {
    41	  const store = new Map(); // path -> string content
    42	  const dirs = new Set(['/']);
    43	  const mtimes = new Map();
    44	  const history = [];
    45	  let clock = 0;
    46	
    47	  const snapshot = () => Object.fromEntries(store);
    48	  const record = (op, paths) => history.push({ op, paths, files: snapshot() });
    49	  const touch = (np) => mtimes.set(np, ++clock);
    50	
    51	  const mkdirp = (np) => {
    52	    let cur = '';
    53	    for (const part of np.split('/').filter(Boolean)) {
    54	      cur = cur + '/' + part;
    55	      dirs.add(cur);
    56	    }
    57	  };
    58	
    59	  const ensureParent = (np, syscall) => {
    60	    const parent = dirname(np);
    61	    if (parent === '/' ) return;
    62	    if (!dirs.has(parent)) throw fsError('ENOENT', syscall, np);
    63	  };
    64	
    65	  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(initialFiles)) {
    66	    const np = normalize(p);
    67	    mkdirp(dirname(np));
    68	    store.set(np, String(content));
    69	    touch(np);
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  const fs = {
    73	    readFileSync(p /*, enc */) {
    74	      const np = normalize(p);
    75	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'open', np);
    76	      return store.get(np);
    77	    },
    78	
    79	    writeFileSync(p, data /*, enc */) {
    80	      const np = normalize(p);
    81	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    82	      store.set(np, String(data));
    83	      touch(np);
    84	      record('writeFileSync', [np]);
    85	    },
    86	
    87	    appendFileSync(p, data) {
    88	      const np = normalize(p);
    89	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    90	      store.set(np, (store.get(np) || '') + String(data));
    91	      touch(np);
    92	      record('appendFileSync', [np]);
    93	    },
    94	
    95	    renameSync(from, to) {
    96	      const nf = normalize(from);
    97	      const nt = normalize(to);
    98	      if (store.has(nf)) {
    99	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   100	        const content = store.get(nf);
   101	        store.delete(nf);
   102	        store.set(nt, content);
   103	        touch(nt);
   104	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   105	        return;
   106	      }
   107	      // Directory rename (atomic move of the node and all descendants) — the
   108	      // lock's dead-reclaim arbitration renames the stale lock dir aside.
   109	      if (dirs.has(nf)) {
   110	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   111	        const pre = nf + '/';
   112	        dirs.delete(nf);
   113	        dirs.add(nt);
   114	        for (const k of [...store.keys()]) {
   115	          if (k.startsWith(pre)) {
   116	            store.set(nt + k.slice(nf.length), store.get(k));
   117	            store.delete(k);
   118	          }
   119	        }
   120	        for (const d of [...dirs]) {
   121	          if (d !== nt && d.startsWith(pre)) {
   122	            dirs.add(nt + d.slice(nf.length));
   123	            dirs.delete(d);
   124	          }
   125	        }
   126	        touch(nt);
   127	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   128	        return;
   129	      }
   130	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rename', nf);
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    mkdirSync(p, opts = {}) {
   134	      const np = normalize(p);
   135	      if (opts.recursive) {
   136	        mkdirp(np);
   137	        record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   138	        return undefined;
   139	      }
   140	      ensureParent(np, 'mkdir');
   141	      if (dirs.has(np) || store.has(np)) throw fsError('EEXIST', 'mkdir', np);
   142	      dirs.add(np);
   143	      record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   144	      return undefined;
   145	    },
   146	
   147	    existsSync(p) {
   148	      const np = normalize(p);
   149	      return store.has(np) || dirs.has(np);
   150	    },
   151	
   152	    readdirSync(p) {
   153	      const np = normalize(p);
   154	      if (!dirs.has(np)) {
   155	        if (store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOTDIR', 'scandir', np);
   156	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'scandir', np);
   157	      }
   158	      const names = new Set();
   159	      const collect = (k) => {
   160	        if (k !== np && dirname(k) === np) names.add(basename(k, np));
   161	      };
   162	      for (const k of store.keys()) collect(k);
   163	      for (const d of dirs) collect(d);
   164	      return [...names];
   165	    },
   166	
   167	    unlinkSync(p) {
   168	      const np = normalize(p);
   169	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'unlink', np);
   170	      store.delete(np);
   171	      record('unlinkSync', [np]);
   172	    },
   173	
   174	    rmSync(p, opts = {}) {
   175	      const np = normalize(p);
   176	      const isFile = store.has(np);
   177	      const isDir = dirs.has(np);
   178	      if (!isFile && !isDir) {
   179	        if (opts.force) return;
   180	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rm', np);
   181	      }
   182	      if (isFile) store.delete(np);
   183	      if (isDir) {
   184	        const pre = np + '/';
   185	        if (!opts.recursive) {
   186	          const hasChildren = [...store.keys(), ...dirs].some((k) => k !== np && k.startsWith(pre));
   187	          if (hasChildren) throw fsError('ENOTEMPTY', 'rmdir', np);
   188	          dirs.delete(np);
   189	        } else {
   190	          for (const k of [...store.keys()]) if (k === np || k.startsWith(pre)) store.delete(k);
   191	          for (const d of [...dirs]) if (d === np || d.startsWith(pre)) dirs.delete(d);
   192	        }
   193	      }
   194	      record('rmSync', [np]);
   195	    },
   196	
   197	    statSync(p) {
   198	      const np = normalize(p);
   199	      if (store.has(np)) {
   200	        const size = Buffer.byteLength(store.get(np));
   201	        return { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   202	      }
   203	      if (dirs.has(np)) {
   204	        return { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size: 0, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   205	      }
   206	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'stat', np);
   207	    },
   208	
   209	    // The fake cannot represent symlinks, so lstat === stat and realpath is
   210	    // normalization. Symlink-refusal behavior is proven over the real fs in
   211	    // tests/integration/symlink-escape.test.mjs.
   212	    lstatSync(p) {
   213	      return fs.statSync(p);
   214	    },
   215	
   216	    realpathSync(p) {
   217	      const np = normalize(p);
   218	      if (!store.has(np) && !dirs.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'realpath', np);
   219	      return np;
   220	    },
   221	
   222	    copyFileSync(src, dest) {
   223	      const ns = normalize(src);
   224	      const nd = normalize(dest);
   225	      if (!store.has(ns)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'copyfile', ns);
   226	      ensureParent(nd, 'copyfile');
   227	      store.set(nd, store.get(ns));
   228	      touch(nd);
   229	      record('copyFileSync', [ns, nd]);
   230	    },
   231	  };

   234	  fs.__history = history;
   235	
   236	  return { fs, files: () => snapshot() };
   237	}

+    const realWrite = io.fs.writeFileSync.bind(io.fs);
+    const realMkdir = io.fs.mkdirSync.bind(io.fs);
+    const realRename = io.fs.renameSync.bind(io.fs);
+    const COMPETITOR = JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 55555, startTime: 222, runId: 'competitor' });
+
+    // The competitor wins the lock the INSTANT before the supervisor's own write
+    // to LOCK lands — injected at whichever primitive the acquire uses to create
+    // it. NO readFileSync hook, so a wx/mkdir-FIRST acquire (which never reads
+    // before creating) cannot dodge the race.
+    let injected = false;
+    const injectBefore = (/** @type {any} */ target) => {
+      if (injected || String(target) !== LOCK) return;
+      injected = true;
+      realMkdir(DIR, { recursive: true });
+      realWrite(LOCK, COMPETITOR);
+    };
+
+    // Exclusive create (flag wx/ax) enforces the O_EXCL semantics memfs lacks:
+    // once the competitor exists, an exclusive write throws EEXIST.
+    io.fs.writeFileSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ data, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
+      const flag = typeof opts === 'string' ? undefined : opts?.flag;
+      const exclusive = flag === 'wx' || flag === 'ax' || flag === 'wx+' || flag === 'ax+';
+      injectBefore(p);
+      if (exclusive && String(p) === LOCK && io.fs.existsSync(p)) throw Object.assign(new Error(`EEXIST: file already exists, open '${p}'`), { code: 'EEXIST' });
+      return realWrite(p, data, opts); // a PLAIN write to LOCK clobbers the competitor
+    };
+    // mkdirSync(LOCK) as an exclusive lock dir: memfs already throws EEXIST when a
+    // node exists at the path (the injected competitor file).
+    io.fs.mkdirSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ opts) => { injectBefore(p); return realMkdir(p, opts); };
+    // A tmp+rename publish (atomicWriteJson) clobbers the competitor on rename.
+    io.fs.renameSync = (/** @type {any} */ from, /** @type {any} */ to) => { injectBefore(to); return realRename(from, to); };
+
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 1, 'a wx/mkdir-first exclusive acquire observes EEXIST and refuses the run');
+    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned when the lock was lost to the competitor');
+    assert.match(JSON.parse(io.files()[LOCK]).runId, /competitor/, "the competitor's lock is not clobbered (a check-then-write path fails here)");
+  });
+});
+
+// ===========================================================================
+// G4 (A4) — record in-flight child groups; kill recorded orphans on reclaim.
+describe('loop run — child-group recording + kill-on-reclaim (G4)', () => {
+  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
+
+  const readChildren = (io) => {
+    const raw = io.files()[`${DIR}/children.ndjson`];
+    if (typeof raw !== 'string') return [];
+    return raw.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== '').map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
+  };
+
+  it('RED (G4): a child-start record {childId, pid, pgid, startedAt} exists on disk WHILE the child is in flight (before completion)', async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    let n = 0;
+    const inflightOk = [];
+    // The runner reports the child's group via opts.onStart (spawn time); the
+    // supervisor must persist the record synchronously in that handler — BEFORE
+    // the child completes — so a mid-run crash leaves the orphan reap-able.
+    io.superviseChild = (/** @type {any} */ _spec, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
+      n += 1;

+    };

+  });

+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/children.ndjson`, JSON.stringify({ childId: '001-plan', pid: 8001, pgid: 9001, startedAt: T0 }) + '\n');

    29	// Flags that consume a value — needed to split positionals from flag tokens
    30	// without mistaking a flag's value for a positional. Keep in sync with the
    31	// strict spec below plus the implied common string flag (--root).
    32	const STRING_FLAGS = new Set(['root', 'approve-smoke']);
    33	
    34	/**
    35	 * `baton loop <subcommand>` — the Layer-2 goal-loop surface. v1 subcommands:
    36	 * `init "<goal>"` (scaffold loop.json; two-phase, idempotent, dry-run-able).
    37	 * Supervisor-side by design: the BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD guard deliberately
    38	 * does NOT apply here.
    39	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    40	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    41	 */
    42	export async function cmdLoop(args, io) {
    43	  /** @type {string[]} */
    44	  const positionals = [];
    45	  /** @type {string[]} */
    46	  const flagTokens = [];
    47	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    48	    const a = args[i];
    49	    if (typeof a === 'string' && a.startsWith('--')) {
    50	      flagTokens.push(a);
    51	      if (STRING_FLAGS.has(a.slice(2)) && i + 1 < args.length) {
    52	        i += 1;
    53	        flagTokens.push(args[i]);
    54	      }
    55	    } else {
    56	      positionals.push(a);
    57	    }
    58	  }
    59	
    60	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(flagTokens, { 'dry-run': 'boolean', 'approve-smoke': 'string' });
    61	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'loop', parsed.error);
    62	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    63	
    64	  const sub = positionals[0];
    65	  if (sub === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', 'a subcommand is required — try: baton loop init "<goal>" or baton loop run');
    66	  if (sub === 'run') {
    67	    if (positionals.length > 1) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `unexpected argument '${positionals[1]}'`);
    68	    if (flags['approve-smoke'] === '') return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', '--approve-smoke requires a value (the token from smoke-approval.json)');
    69	    return runLoop(flags, io);
    70	  }
    71	  if (sub !== 'init') return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `unknown subcommand '${sub}' (supported: init, run)`);
    72	
    73	  const goal = positionals[1];
    74	  if (goal === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', 'loop init requires a goal — baton loop init "<goal>"');
    75	  if (positionals.length > 2) return usageError(io, flags, 'loop', `unexpected argument '${positionals[2]}'`);
    76	
    77	  const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    78	  const path = `${root}/loop.json`;
    79	
    80	  const finish = (/** @type {any} */ data, /** @type {string} */ human) => {
    81	    if (flags.json) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data });
    82	    else io.stdout.write(`${human}\n`);
    83	    return 0;
    84	  };
    85	
    86	  // Idempotent: an existing spec is the user's — never overwritten.
    87	  if (io.fs.existsSync(path)) {
    88	    return finish({ created: false, path }, `baton loop init: ${path} already exists — left untouched`);
    89	  }
    90	  if (flags['dry-run'] === true) {
    91	    return finish(
    92	      { created: false, path, dryRun: true },
    93	      `baton loop init (dry-run): would write ${path} — the default baton/loop@1 spec with goal "${goal}"`,
    94	    );
    95	  }
    96	  ensureDir(io.fs, root);
    97	  atomicWriteJson(io.fs, path, defaultLoopSpec(goal));
    98	  return finish({ created: true, path }, `baton loop init: wrote ${path} (goal "${goal}") — edit constraints/smoke, then run baton loop run`);
    99	}

   269	            `You are the ${phase.role} for loop run ${runId}, phase '${phase.id}'.`,
   270	            `Goal: ${spec.goal}`,
   271	            spec.constraints.length > 0 ? `Constraints:\n${spec.constraints.map((/** @type {string} */ c) => `- ${c}`).join('\n')}` : '',
   272	            findings ? `The previous attempt was BLOCKED with these findings — address every one:\n${findings}` : '',
   273	            "End with exactly:\nVERDICT: APPROVED | APPROVED_WITH_NOTES | BLOCKED\nFINDINGS: numbered findings, or 'none'",
   274	          ]
   275	            .filter(Boolean)
   276	            .join('\n\n');
   277	        forcedAssignment = null;
   278	        forcedPrompt = null;
   279	
   280	        childSeq += 1;
   281	        const logPath = `${p.dir}/children/${String(childSeq).padStart(3, '0')}-${phase.id}.log`;
   282	        ensureDir(io.fs, `${p.dir}/children`);
   283	        const childSpec = buildChildArgv(assignment, prompt, { root });
   284	        const result = await runner(childSpec, {
   285	          timeoutMs: spec.budgets.perRoleTimeoutMin * 60_000,
   286	          graceMs: 10_000,
   287	          logPath,
   288	          maxLogBytes: 1_000_000,
   289	          platform: assignment.platform,
   290	        });
   291	
   292	        // Classification reads the child's LOG (the frozen transcript), so a
   293	        // limit banner routes to failover even when a verdict parsed.
   294	        const transcript = io.fs.existsSync(logPath) ? io.fs.readFileSync(logPath, 'utf8') : '';
   295	        const cls = classify({ text: transcript, exitCode: result.exitCode ?? 0, platform: assignment.platform, table }).class;
   296	
   297	        if (cls === 'usage-limit' || cls === 'model-unavailable' || cls === 'other-error' || cls === 'throttle' || cls === 'auth') {
   298	          failoverAttempt += 1;
   299	          const decision = await runFailover({
   300	            root,
   301	            io,
   302	            config,
   303	            role: phase.role,
   304	            assignment,
   305	            transcript,
   306	            exitCode: result.exitCode ?? 1,
   307	            sessionHint,
   308	            probes: null,
   309	            avoid,
   310	            avoidEntries,
   311	            attempt: failoverAttempt,
   312	            loopState: { runId, phaseIndex: state.phaseIndex, iterations: state.iterations, status: state.status },
   313	          });
   314	          if (decision.action === 'park') {
   315	            await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PARK, reason: decision.reason });
   316	            io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: parked — ${decision.reason}${'resumeAt' in decision && decision.resumeAt ? ` (resume around ${decision.resumeAt})` : ''}\n`);
   317	            return EXIT_PARKED;
   318	          }
   319	          if (decision.action === 'relaunch') {
   320	            // Platform avoidance is PER-DEATH, not cumulative: runFailover
   321	            // already avoided the just-died platform when it resolved the
   322	            // relaunch. Accumulating here would exhaust every platform on a
   323	            // chained A→B→A failover and park instead of returning to A
   324	            // (acceptance constraint 2). Entry-level avoidance (dead MODELS)
   325	            // does accumulate — a rejected model stays rejected.
   326	            if (Array.isArray(decision.avoidEntries)) avoidEntries = decision.avoidEntries;
   327	            forcedAssignment = { ...decision.assignment, role: phase.role };
   328	            forcedPrompt = typeof decision.prompt === 'string' && decision.prompt.length > 0 ? decision.prompt : null;
   329	            continue; // relaunch the same phase on the new assignment
   330	          }
   331	          continue; // retry: re-spawn the same phase once more
   332	        }
   333	
   334	        const verdict = String(result.verdict ?? 'BLOCKED');
   335	        if (verdict === 'APPROVED' || verdict === 'APPROVED_WITH_NOTES') {
   336	          await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE });
   337	          phaseDone = true;
   338	
   339	          // The smoke gate fires right after the smoke-build phase completes.
   340	          if (phase.id === 'smoke-build' && typeof spec.smoke?.cmd === 'string' && spec.smoke.cmd.length > 0) {
   341	            const [cmd, ...cmdArgs] = spec.smoke.cmd.split(/\s+/);
   342	            let smokeOut = '';
   343	            try {
   344	              const r = await io.execFile(cmd, cmdArgs, { cwd: root });
   345	              smokeOut = `${r?.stdout ?? ''}${r?.stderr ?? ''}`;
   346	            } catch (err) {
   347	              smokeOut = `smoke command failed: ${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? String(err)}`;
   348	            }
   349	            await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_AWAIT });
   350	            const git = await gitSnapshot({ execFile: io.execFile, cwd: root, fs: io.fs });
   351	            const inputs = {
   352	              stateDigest: dedupeKey(io.fs.readFileSync(p.state, 'utf8')),
   353	              smokeCmd: spec.smoke.cmd,
   354	              smokeOutputDigest: dedupeKey(smokeOut),
   355	              gitHead: gitHeadOf(git),
   356	              gitContentDigest: gitContentOf(git),
   357	              childAssignment: JSON.stringify({ platform: assignment.platform, model: assignment.model }),
   358	            };
   359	            const token = smokeApprovalToken(inputs);
   360	            atomicWriteJson(io.fs, `${p.dir}/smoke-approval.json`, { token, inputs, issuedAt: io.now() });
   361	            atomicWriteText(
   362	              io.fs,
   363	              `${p.dir}/SMOKE-REVIEW.md`,
   364	              `# Smoke gate — human approval required\n\nRun: ${runId}\nCommand: \`${spec.smoke.cmd}\`\nExpected: ${spec.smoke.expect ?? '(unspecified)'}\n\n## Output\n\n\`\`\`\n${smokeOut}\n\`\`\`\n\nApprove with:\n\n    baton loop run --approve-smoke ${token}\n\nThe token is bound to the current state/output/git — any drift refuses it.\n`,
   365	            );
   366	            io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: smoke gate reached — review ${p.dir}/SMOKE-REVIEW.md and approve with --approve-smoke <token>\n`);
   367	            return 0;
   368	          }
   369	        } else {
   370	          // BLOCKED (or unparseable, already coerced to BLOCKED upstream).
   371	          findings = String(result.findings ?? '');
   372	          const after = await transition({ type: LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION, gate: phase.id, verdict });
   373	          if (after.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) {
   374	            atomicWriteText(
   375	              io.fs,
   376	              `${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md`,
   377	              `# Loop escalation\n\nGate '${phase.id}' exhausted its ${cap}-iteration cap on run ${runId}.\nLast findings:\n\n${findings}\n`,
   378	            );
   379	            io.stderr.write(`baton loop run: gate '${phase.id}' escalated at the cap — see ${p.dir}/ESCALATION.md\n`);
   380	            return EXIT_ESCALATED;
   381	          }
   382	        }
   383	      }
   384	    }
   385	
   386	    if (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.DONE || state.phaseIndex >= state.phaseCount) {
   387	      io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: done — every phase completed (run ${runId})\n`);
   388	      return 0;
   389	    }
   390	    io.stdout.write(`baton loop run: stopped in status ${state.status}\n`);
   391	    return state.status === LOOP_STATUS.PARKED ? EXIT_PARKED : state.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED ? EXIT_ESCALATED : 0;
   392	  } finally {
   393	    // The lock is per-invocation: release on every exit path so a paused run
   394	    // (awaiting approval) can be resumed by the next invocation.
   395	    try {
   396	      if (io.fs.existsSync(lockPath)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lockPath);
   397	    } catch {
   398	      // Best effort — a stale lock is recoverable via the provably-dead path.
   399	    }
   400	  }
   401	}

    13	
    14	/**
    15	 * Rename with retry on EPERM (Windows AV/indexer lock pattern): 3 attempts total.
    16	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} from @param {string} to
    17	 */
    18	function renameWithRetry(fs, from, to) {
    19	  let lastErr;
    20	  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 3; attempt += 1) {
    21	    try {
    22	      fs.renameSync(from, to);
    23	      return;
    24	    } catch (err) {
    25	      lastErr = err;
    26	      if (!err || /** @type {any} */ (err).code !== 'EPERM') throw err;
    27	    }
    28	  }
    29	  throw lastErr;
    30	}
    31	
    32	/**
    33	 * Write text atomically: tmp sibling + rename. The live target is never
    34	 * truncated or partially written.
    35	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path @param {string} content
    36	 */
    37	export function atomicWriteText(fs, path, content) {
    38	  const tmp = tmpPathFor(path);
    39	  fs.writeFileSync(tmp, content);
    40	  try {
    41	    renameWithRetry(fs, tmp, path);
    42	  } catch (err) {
    43	    try {
    44	      fs.unlinkSync(tmp);
    45	    } catch {
    46	      // leftover tmp is harmless; cleanupTmp removes it later
    47	    }
    48	    throw err;
    49	  }
    50	}
    51	
    52	/**
    53	 * Atomically write pretty-printed JSON (2-space, trailing newline).
    54	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path @param {unknown} obj
    55	 */
    56	export function atomicWriteJson(fs, path, obj) {
    57	  atomicWriteText(fs, path, JSON.stringify(obj, null, 2) + '\n');
    58	}
    59	
    60	/**
    61	 * Read + parse JSON without ever throwing.
    62	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path
    63	 * @returns {{ok: true, value: any} | {ok: false, error: string}}
    64	 */
    65	export function safeReadJson(fs, path) {
    66	  let raw;
    67	  try {
    68	    raw = fs.readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
    69	  } catch (err) {
    70	    return { ok: false, error: `read failed: ${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code ?? err}` };
    71	  }
    72	  try {
    73	    return { ok: true, value: JSON.parse(raw) };
    74	  } catch (err) {
    75	    return { ok: false, error: `parse failed: ${/** @type {any} */ (err)?.message ?? err}` };
    76	  }
    77	}
    78	
    79	/**
    80	 * Copy the current content to <path>.bak (when present), then write atomically.
    81	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path @param {string} content
    82	 */
    83	export function backupThenWrite(fs, path, content) {
    84	  if (fs.existsSync(path)) {
    85	    fs.copyFileSync(path, `${path}.bak`);
    86	  }
    87	  atomicWriteText(fs, path, content);
    88	}
    89	
    90	/**
    91	 * Remove leftover *.tmp.* files in a directory (crash debris).
    92	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} dir
    93	 */
    94	export function cleanupTmp(fs, dir) {
    95	  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) return;
    96	  for (const name of fs.readdirSync(dir)) {
    97	    if (/\.tmp\./.test(name)) {
    98	      try {
    99	        fs.unlinkSync(`${dir}/${name}`);
   100	      } catch {
   101	        // already gone or locked; not fatal
   102	      }
   103	    }
   104	  }
   105	}
   106	
   107	/**
   108	 * @param {any} fs @param {string} path
   109	 */
   110	export function ensureDir(fs, path) {
   111	  fs.mkdirSync(path, { recursive: true });
   112	}
